=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given integers W,H,L,R,D,U.
A town of Kyoto is on the two-dimensional plane.
In the town, there is exactly one block at each lattice point (x,y) that satisfies all of the following conditions. There are no blocks at any other points.

- 0\leq x\leq W
- 0\leq y\leq H
- x<L or R<x or y<D or U<y

Snuke traveled through the town as follows.

- First, he chooses one block and stands there.
- Then, he performs the following operation any number of times (possibly zero):
- Move one unit in the positive direction of the x-axis or the positive direction of the y-axis. However, the point after moving must also have a block.



Print the number, modulo 998244353, of possible paths that Snuke could have taken.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
W H L R D U

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 0\leq L\leq R\leq W\leq 10^6
- 0\leq D\leq U\leq H\leq 10^6
- There is at least one block.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

4 3 1 2 2 3

Sample Output 1

192

The following are examples of possible paths. Here, a path is represented by listing the lattice points visited in order.

- (3,0)
- (0,0)\rightarrow (1,0)\rightarrow (2,0)\rightarrow (2,1)\rightarrow (3,1)\rightarrow (3,2)\rightarrow (4,2)\rightarrow (4,3)
- (0,1)\rightarrow (0,2)

There are 192 possible paths.

Sample Input 2

10 12 4 6 8 11

Sample Output 2

4519189

Sample Input 3

192 25 0 2 0 9

Sample Output 3

675935675

Do not forget to print the number of paths modulo 998244353.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

However, before settle, need ensure invalid formula counts all invalid paths exactly. Let's formalize.

Full grid S all lattice points in [0,W]x[0,H]. Forbidden A = [L,R]x[D,U] (closed). We want count paths in B = S\A. Total full paths T = sum_{p in S} F[p]? Actually T = sum_{p in S} dp_full[p] = sum F[x][y]. Valid = T - invalid, where invalid = number of full-grid paths (monotone, any start/end) that visit at least one point of A.

For any invalid path, let p be the first point of A visited. If p is the starting point, then prefix is empty. Otherwise, the step before p is from outside A to p. Since moves are east/north and A is a rectangle:
- If p.x > L and p.y > D? Could it be first? If p.x>L and p.y>D, then both left neighbor (p.x-1,p.y) and below neighbor (p.x,p.y-1) are in A (because x-1>=L, y>=D; x>=L, y-1>=D), so any predecessor would be in A, impossible for first. Thus first point must have p.x=L or p.y=D (or both). Good.
- If p.y=D, p.x in [L,R], last step could be from below (p.x,D-1) if D>0. If p.x>L, left neighbor (p.x-1,D) is in A, so last step cannot be from left. If p.x=L, last step could be from left (L-1,D) if L>0, or from below. If p.x=L and p.y>D, below neighbor (L,y-1) in A, so last step from left. If p.y=D and p.x=L, both possible. Thus first-hit boundary bottom and left as above.

For p on bottom edge (x,D), x in [L,R], entering from below. Prefix is any full-grid path from any start s to predecessor q=(x,D-1) that avoids A before p. Since q.y=D-1 < D, any path to q has y<=D-1, so it cannot visit A (which has y>=D). Thus all full-grid paths ending at q in the rectangle [0..x]x[0..D-1]? Wait F[x][D-1] counts paths from any start s with 0<=s.x<=x, 0<=s.y<=D-1 to q=(x,D-1) in full grid. Since q.x=x, start x<=x; q.y=D-1, start y<=D-1. All such paths stay within [0..x]x[0..D-1] (monotone), so avoid A. Number = F[x][D-1]. Then step up to p. This is valid prefix. For x=L, from below also F[L][D-1]. Note if D=0, q.y=-1 nonexistent, F=0.

For p on left edge (L,y), y in [D,U], entering from left. Prefix q=(L-1,y). Since q.x=L-1<L, any path to q has x<=L-1, avoids A. Number = F[L-1][y]. Then step right. For y=D, from left also F[L-1][D]. If L=0, F=0.

Thus number of outside-entry invalid paths with first hit p is:
- bottom: F[x][D-1] * E(W-x,H-D) for x=L..R.
- left: F[L-1][y] * E(W-L,H-y) for y=D..U.
No double count because first hit p and last step direction unique; for corner, bottom and left contributions correspond to different last steps, and a path has exactly one last step into p, so disjoint.

Start-inside invalid paths: For each p=(x,y) in A, path starts at p. Number of continuations from p to any end = E(W-x,H-y). This includes path of length 0. These paths are not counted in outside-entry because start not outside. Sum over p in A.

Then invalid = S_start + S_bottom + S_left.

But note F[x][y] = dp_full ending at (x,y) = number of paths from any start to (x,y). For x,y within full grid. It is C(x+y+2,x+1)-1. Let's verify for x=0,y=0: C(2,1)-1=1. Good. For x=0,y=2: C(4,1)-1=3. Good.

E(a,b) = number of paths starting at a point with remaining width a, height b to any end. Formula same C(a+b+2,a+1)-1. Check a=0,b=0: C(2,1)-1=1. Good.

But we can optimize by computing bottom and left sums with loops. Need be careful modulo and negative.

Pseudo:

import sys
MOD=998244353
W,H,L,R,D,U=map(int, sys.stdin.readline().split())
max_n = W+H+4
fact=[1]*(max_n+1)
for i in range(1,max_n+1): fact[i]=fact[i-1]*i%MOD
invfact=[1]*(max_n+1)
invfact[max_n]=pow(fact[max_n], MOD-2, MOD)
for i in range(max_n,0,-1): invfact[i-1]=invfact[i]*i%MOD

def C(n,k):
    if k<0 or k>n: return 0
    return fact[n]*invfact[k]%MOD*invfact[n-k]%MOD

def F(x,y):
    if x<0 or y<0: return 0
    return (C(x+y+2, x+1) - 1) % MOD

def E(a,b):
    if a<0 or b<0: return 0
    return (C(a+b+2, a+1) - 1) % MOD

total = (C(W+H+4, W+2) - (W+H+4) - (W+1)*(H+1)) % MOD

invalid = 0

# start inside
# sum_{x=L..R} sum_{y=D..U} E(W-x, H-y)
# Could loop double? Need avoid O(area) which can be 1e12. Need compute double sum efficiently. Ah! Important. Start_inside sum over all points in A, area up to (R-L+1)*(U-D+1) up to 1e12, cannot loop double. Need derive closed form or compute as sum over x of sum_y E(W-x,H-y). Need O(W+H) maybe by precomputing suffix sums over y for each x? But x range up to 1e6, y range up to 1e6, double impossible. Need compute double sum in O(W+H) or O(1). We need handle start_inside efficiently.

We need sum_{x=L..R} sum_{y=D..U} E(W-x, H-y).

E(a,b) = C(a+b+2, a+1) - 1. Let a = W-x, b = H-y. As x increases, a decreases; y increases, b decreases. Sum over rectangle in (a,b): a from W-R to W-L, b from H-U to H-D. Need sum of C(a+b+2, a+1) minus area.

So S_start = sum_{a=A1}^{A2} sum_{b=B1}^{B2} [ C(a+b+2, a+1) - 1 ], where A1=W-R, A2=W-L, B1=H-U, B2=H-D. Note A1<=A2, B1<=B2.

Need compute double sum of binomial over rectangular range. There is identity for prefix sums of binomial over both dimensions:
Sum_{a=0..A} sum_{b=0..B} C(a+b+2, a+1) maybe can be expressed in closed form. Let's derive. Then rectangle sum by inclusion-exclusion of prefix sums.

We already derived S1 = sum_{x=0..W} sum_{y=0..H} C(x+y+2,x+1) = C(W+H+4,W+2) - (W+H+4). More generally, for a up to A, b up to B:
S(A,B) = sum_{a=0..A} sum_{b=0..B} C(a+b+2, a+1).
Let i=a+1 (1..A+1), j=b+1 (1..B+1). S = sum_{i=1}^{A+1} sum_{j=1}^{B+1} C(i+j, i).
Using identity above: S = C(A+B+4, A+2) - (A+B+4)? Wait earlier for W,H: S1 = C(W+H+4,W+2) - (W+H+4). Replacing A=W,B=H gives C(A+B+4,A+2) - (A+B+4). Let's verify for A=0,B=0: S should C(2,1)=2. Formula C(4,2)-4=6-4=2. Good. For A=1,B=0: sum a=0..1,b=0: C(2,1)+C(3,2)=2+3=5. Formula C(5,3)-5=10-5=5. Good. So prefix sum of C part = C(A+B+4, A+2) - (A+B+4).

Then sum_{a=0..A,b=0..B} E(a,b) = S(A,B) - (A+1)(B+1) = C(A+B+4,A+2) - (A+B+4) - (A+1)(B+1). This is exactly full total for a grid of width A, height B? Yes.

Thus rectangle sum can be computed via 2D prefix inclusion-exclusion:
Pref(A,B) = sum_{a=0..A,b=0..B} E(a,b) = C(A+B+4,A+2) - (A+B+4) - (A+1)(B+1).

Then sum_{a=A1..A2, b=B1..B2} E(a,b) = Pref(A2,B2) - Pref(A1-1,B2) - Pref(A2,B1-1) + Pref(A1-1,B1-1), with Pref(-1,*)=0 etc. Great! So start_inside O(1).

Need ensure A1=W-R, A2=W-L, B1=H-U, B2=H-D. Since L<=R, A1<=A2; D<=U, B1<=B2. All nonnegative because R<=W,U<=H. Good.

Let's test sample1 start_inside with Pref. W=4,H=3,L=1,R=2,D=2,U=3. A1=2,A2=3,B1=0,B2=1. Pref(3,1) = C(8,5)? A+B+4=8, A+2=5, C=56 -8 - (4*2=8)=40? Wait formula C(8,5)=56, minus (A+B+4=8), minus (A+1)(B+1)=4*2=8 =>40. Sum E for a=0..3,b=0..1. Let's compute E: b=0: a=0 1,a=1 2,a=2 3,a=3 4 sum10; b=1: a=0 E(0,1)=2? C(3,1)-1=2; a=1 E(1,1)=C(4,2)-1=5; a=2 E(2,1)=9; a=3 E(3,1)=14 sum30; total40. Good.
Rectangle a=2..3,b=0..1: E(2,0)=3,E(3,0)=4,E(2,1)=9,E(3,1)=14 sum30. Pref(3,1)=40 - Pref(1,1)? A1-1=1,B2=1: Pref(1,1)=C(6,3)-6 - (2*2=4)=20-6-4=10? E a0..1,b0..1:1+2+2+5=10. - Pref(3,-1)=0 + Pref(1,-1)=0 =>30. Good.

So start_inside O(1).

Now bottom sum: sum_{x=L}^{R} F[x][D-1] * E(W-x, H-D). Need compute efficiently. Range length up to 1e6, loop okay. But maybe can derive closed form? Loop okay. Similarly left sum loop up to 1e6. Total loops 2e6. Fine.

But wait bottom sum uses F[x][D-1] for x from L to R. If D=0, F=0 for all, skip. If D>0, loop x. E term depends on x. Need compute each O(1) with C. 1e6 iterations okay. Left sum loop y from D to U if L>0. 1e6 iterations. Combined 2e6. Factorial precompute 2e6. Total 4e6 operations, fine in Python.

Could optimize by precomputing arrays? Not needed.

Need also consider if L=0, left sum skip because F[-1][y]=0. If R? bottom skip if D=0. If D=0 and U=H? hole touches bottom/top? Start_inside still. But if D=0, bottom entry from below impossible. If L=0, left entry impossible. Good.

S_bottom = ∑_{x=L}^{R} (A_x - 1)(B_x - 1) = ∑ A_x B_x - ∑ A_x - ∑ B_x + len, where A_x = C(x+D+1, D), B_x = C(W+H-D-x+2, H-D+1).

∑ A_x over x=L..R can be computed by hockey-stick: ∑_{x=L}^{R} C(x+D+1, D) = ∑_{i=L+1}^{R+1} C(i+D, D) = C(R+1+D+1, D+1) - C(L+D+1, D+1)? Let's derive: ∑_{i=0}^{N} C(i+D, D) = C(N+D+1, D+1). So ∑_{i=L+1}^{R+1} = C(R+1+D+1, D+1) - C(L+D+1, D+1) = C(R+D+2, D+1) - C(L+D+1, D+1). Good.

∑ B_x over x=L..R: B_x = C(W+H-D-x+2, H-D+1). Let j = W+H-D-x+2. As x increases, j decreases. Could use hockey-stick with variable. Let B_x = C(j, K) where K=H-D+1. Sum over x=L..R of C(W+H-D-x+2, K). Let t = W+H-D+2 - x. When x=L, t_max = W+H-D+2 - L; x=R, t_min = W+H-D+2 - R. Sum_{t=t_min}^{t_max} C(t, K) = ∑_{t=K}^{t_max} C(t,K) - ∑_{t=K}^{t_min-1} C(t,K) = C(t_max+1, K+1) - C(t_min, K+1). Since ∑_{t=K}^{N} C(t,K)=C(N+1,K+1). Good.

∑ A_x B_x is the convolution full/partial. Maybe can compute via Vandermonde partial? Let's derive partial sum identity.

We need S_AB(L,R) = ∑_{x=L}^{R} C(x+D+1, D) * C(W+H-D-x+2, H-D+1).

Let i = x+1. Then first C(i+D, D). Second C(W+H-D-i+3, H-D+1). Let r=D, t=H-D+1, S=W+2? As above second = C(t+S-i, t) with S=W+2? Check t+S-i = H-D+1 + W+2 - (x+1) = W+H-D-x+2? Compute H-D+1+W+2-x-1 = W+H-D-x+2. Yes. Good. So S_AB = ∑_{i=L+1}^{R+1} C(r+i, r) * C(t+S-i, t).

Identity for full sum i=0..S: ∑_{i=0}^{S} C(r+i, r) C(t+S-i, t) = C(r+t+S+1, r+t+1). This is Vandermonde: choose? Let's verify small r=t=0,S=1: i=0 C0,0*C1,0=1; i=1 C1,0*C0,0=1 total2. RHS C(0+0+1+1=2? r+t+S+1=2, r+t+1=1) C(2,1)=2. Good.

Partial sum up to n (n<=S): P(n) = ∑_{i=0}^{n} C(r+i,r) C(t+S-i,t). Is there closed form? It is the probability distribution of negative hypergeometric; CDF can be expressed as 1 - something with a single binomial? Let's derive using generating functions.

C(r+i,r) = [z^i] 1/(1-z)^{r+1}. C(t+S-i,t) = [z^{S-i}] 1/(1-z)^{t+1}? Actually C(t+m,t) coefficient of z^m in 1/(1-z)^{t+1}. Product sum i=0..n is coefficient of z^S in (1/(1-z)^{r+1})(1/(1-z)^{t+1}) truncated to terms with first factor degree <=n? Not simple.

But there is identity:
∑_{i=0}^{n} C(r+i,r) C(t+S-i,t) = C(r+t+S+1, r+t+1) - ∑_{i=n+1}^{S} ... 
The tail maybe also not single.

Maybe use symmetry and cumulative binomial: P(n) = C(r+t+S+1, S-n-1?)? Let's test.

Now, let's decide final code structure:

```
import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    W,H,L,R,D,U = map(int, sys.stdin.readline().split())
    max_n = W + H + 4
    fact = [1] * (max_n + 1)
    for i in range(1, max_n + 1):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1] * i % MOD
    invfact = [1] * (max_n + 1)
    invfact[max_n] = pow(fact[max_n], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(max_n, 0, -1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i] * i % MOD

    def C(n,k):
        if k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def pref(A,B):
        if A < 0 or B < 0:
            return 0
        return (C(A+B+4, A+2) - (A+B+4) - (A+1)*(B+1)) % MOD

    total = pref(W,H)

    A1 = W - R
    A2 = W - L
    B1 = H - U
    B2 = H - D
    start_inside = (pref(A2,B2) - pref(A1-1,B2) - pref(A2,B1-1) + pref(A1-1,B1-1)) % MOD

    invalid = start_inside

    if D > 0:
        # bottom edge
        for x in range(L, R+1):
            n1 = x + D + 1
            k1 = x + 1
            f = (C(n1,k1) - 1) % MOD
            n2 = W + H - D - x + 2
            k2 = W - x + 1
            e = (C(n2,k2) - 1) % MOD
            invalid = (invalid + f * e) % MOD

    if L > 0:
        # left edge
        for y in range(D, U+1):
            n1 = L + y + 1
            k1 = L
            f = (C(n1,k1) - 1) % MOD
            n2 = W + H - L - y + 2
            k2 = W - L + 1
            e = (C(n2,k2) - 1) % MOD
            invalid = (invalid + f * e) % MOD

    ans = (total - invalid) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Need check left k1 = L. F[L-1][y] = C(L+y+1, L) -1. Yes. If L>0. n1-k1 = y+1 >=1. Good.
Left n2 = W+H-L-y+2, k2=W-L+1. n2-k2 = H-y+1 >=1. Good.

Potential issue: In bottom loop, if D>0, n1 = x+D+1. For x=0,D=1 => n1=2,k1=1,C=2,F=1? F[0][0]=1 yes. n2 = W+H-1-x+2. For W=H=1,x=0,D=1: n2=1+1-1-0+2=3,k2=2,C(3,2)=3,E(1,0)=2? Wait E(a,b) for bottom p=(0,1) in W=H=1? a=W-x=1,b=H-D=0, E(1,0)=2. Formula C(3,2)-1=2. n2=3,k2=2. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible off-by-one in Pref for start_inside due to E(a,b) = C(a+b+2,a+1)-1 and Pref(A,B) = C(A+B+4,A+2) - (A+B+4) - (A+1)(B+1). Let's prove one more time:
Sum_{a=0..A,b=0..B} C(a+b+2,a+1). Let i=a+1, j=b+1. Sum_{i=1..A+1,j=1..B+1} C(i+j,i).
Identity ∑_{i=0}^{M}∑_{j=0}^{N} C(i+j,i) = C(M+N+2,M+1)-1.
Set M=A+1,N=B+1: total = C(A+B+4,A+2)-1.
Subtract i=0 terms: ∑_{j=0}^{B+1} C(j,0)=B+2.
Subtract j=0 terms with i>=1: ∑_{i=1}^{A+1} C(i,0)=A+1.
No double count because i=0,j=0 already removed in i=0; j=0 terms start i=1. So sum = C(...)-1 - (B+2) - (A+1) = C(...) - (A+B+4). Then subtract (A+1)(B+1) for -1. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is a possible problem with Python's modulo of negative in `f = (C(n1,k1) - 1) % MOD`. If C returns 0? Not possible, but okay. We can also compute `f = C(n1,k1) - 1; if f < 0: f += MOD` to avoid `%` but C nonzero, so if C=0 (not possible) then -1. But C can be 0 mod MOD? n<MOD so no. But using `%` is fine. To optimize, since C(n,k) nonzero mod, we can do:
```
f = C(n1,k1) - 1
if f < 0: f += MOD
```
But C could be MOD-? Actually if C=0 not possible, but if C=0 then f=-1, +MOD = MOD-1, correct. If C>0, f in [0,MOD-2]. This avoids modulo. But C function returns int 0..MOD-1. We can use this for speed. Similarly e. But if C=0, f=MOD-1, okay. Let's implement helper maybe:
```
def comb_minus_one(n,k):
    return (C(n,k) - 1) % MOD
```
Function call overhead. Inline:
```
f = C(n1,k1) - 1
if f < 0: f += MOD
```
Since C never 0, but if C=0, f=-1 -> MOD-1. Good. This saves `% MOD` per binomial. But C function itself returns modulo. We can also inline C to avoid function call.

Maybe write a local inline combination code in loops:
```
fn = fact
invf = invfact
mod = MOD
...
c1 = fn[n1] * invf[k1] % mod * invf[n1-k1] % mod
f = c1 - 1
if f < 0: f += mod
```
This avoids function call. Let's implement loops with local variables for speed.

```
def comb(n,k):
    if k < 0 or k > n:

```

Pseudo final:

```
import sys
MOD=998244353

def main():
    W,H,L,R,D,U = map(int, sys.stdin.readline().split())
    max_n = W + H + 4
    fact = [1]*(max_n+1)
    for i in range(1,max_n+1):
        fact[i] = fact[i-1]*i % MOD
    invfact = [1]*(max_n+1)
    invfact[max_n] = pow(fact[max_n], MOD-2, MOD)
    for i in range(max_n,0,-1):
        invfact[i-1] = invfact[i]*i % MOD

    def C(n,k):
        if k < 0 or k > n:
            return 0
        return fact[n] * invfact[k] % MOD * invfact[n-k] % MOD

    def pref(A,B):
        if A < 0 or B < 0:
            return 0
        return (C(A+B+4, A+2) - (A+B+4) - (A+1)*(B+1)) % MOD

    total = pref(W,H)

    A1 = W - R
    A2 = W - L
    B1 = H - U
    B2 = H - D
    invalid = (pref(A2,B2) - pref(A1-1,B2) - pref(A2,B1-1) + pref(A1-1,B1-1)) % MOD

    # local aliases
    fact_l = fact; invfact_l = invfact; mod = MOD

    if D > 0:
        for x in range(L, R+1):
            n1 = x + D + 1
            k1 = x + 1
            c = fact_l[n1] * invfact_l[k1] % mod * invfact_l[n1-k1] % mod
            f = c - 1
            if f < 0:
                f += mod
            n2 = W + H - D - x + 2
            k2 = W - x + 1
            c = fact_l[n2] * invfact_l[k2] % mod * invfact_l[n2-k2] % mod
            e = c - 1
            if e < 0:
                e += mod
            invalid += f * e
            invalid %= mod

    if L > 0:
        for y in range(D, U+1):
            n1 = L + y + 1
            k1 = L
            c = fact_l[n1] * invfact_l[k1] % mod * invfact_l[n1-k1] % mod
            f = c - 1
            if f < 0:
                f += mod
            n2 = W + H - L - y + 2
            k2 = W - L + 1
            c = fact_l[n2] * invfact_l[k2] % mod * invfact_l[n2-k2] % mod
            e = c - 1
            if e < 0:
                e += mod
            invalid += f * e
            invalid %= mod

    ans = (total - invalid) % mod
    print(ans)
```

But our bottom sum for p counts all paths from outside to q=(x,D-1) then up to p, regardless of whether they later? Wait prefix to q avoids A, so before p no A. If a path had passed through (x-1,D) earlier, it would have y=D and x-1>=L, so A, but prefix to q=(x,D-1) has y<=D-1, so cannot. Thus no overcount. Good.
